**Owner/approver:** ARADUN project owner

**Scope:** Content and asset planning for the complete Phase 1 website, with supporting interface microcopy and Codex handoff. No application code, application initialization, asset production, publication or Step 10 work is performed.  
**Identity:** ARADUN — Technology & Digital Solutions · Build. Automate. Grow.

## 2. Content principles and approval language

Use plain international business English, explain the customer’s problem before the technology, and distinguish intended benefits from guaranteed results. Keep exact offer names, service identities, prices, URLs and phase boundaries. Use one maintained catalog for every price placement and quote selection.

**Readiness codes — editorial register labels only, not new database enums:**

| Code | Required status | Publication treatment |
|---|---|---|
| A | Approved and ready | Source-approved text/fact with required operational verification; still subject to route/package/asset gates |
| D | Drafted and awaiting approval | R9 proposed wording; may be previewed privately, not silently marked production-approved |
| B | Requires verified business information | Missing fact, deliverable, permission, capability or measurement; no invented substitute |
| V | Requires asset creation | Required export/image/diagram or accessibility derivative not supplied/validated |
| L | Requires legal review | Actual entity/data/commercial practices must be reviewed before policy publication |
| F | Deferred to a future phase | Preserve references without activating routes, features or promises |

Multiple codes can apply to a record. Approval of a page template is separate from approval of its actual record, copy, package scope and assets. Every new prose draft in §§3–15 and Appendix A metadata defaults to D unless explicitly quoted as an unchanged approved identity/label. Confirmed catalog prices remain A as source values, but package publication remains B until units, scope and availability are approved.

Do not fabricate clients, reviews, team members, offices, awards, credentials, partnerships, experience durations, availability or outcomes. Public proof classifications are Client Work, ARADUN Internal Product and Concept/Demo. Attribute prior personal/employer work honestly with permission. Labeled SAMPLE/DEMO/PLACEHOLDER material is allowed for private layouts/tests, kept separate from production data. Fictional testimonials and fictitious performance proof cannot publish. Actual concept work can publish only when honestly labeled and reviewed. Missing proof leaves its planned slot tracked and omitted from the public render until eligible; it does not delete the required template.

Content must be useful without animation, visible on mobile, accessible by keyboard, and understandable with images disabled. Important scope, limitations, price periods and consent explanations cannot be hover-only, image-only or hidden in decoration. No manufactured scarcity, fake live counters or unsupported response-time promises.

## 3. Brand messaging and voice

**Confirmed identity:** ARADUN — Technology & Digital Solutions. **Primary tagline:** Build. Automate. Grow. **Initial locale/currency:** English/USD. **Primary action:** Get a Quote.

**Proposed positioning statement — D:** ARADUN connects design, software, automation, infrastructure and digital marketing around the needs of a business. Clients can explore a specific service or discuss a coordinated scope, with requirements, responsibilities and next steps agreed before delivery.

**Short description — D:** ARADUN provides technology and digital services for businesses, spanning software, design, automation, cloud, cybersecurity, marketing and video.

**Extended description — D:** ARADUN — Technology & Digital Solutions helps businesses plan and develop their digital presence, systems and workflows. Our catalog brings together web and mobile development, AI and CRM services, digital marketing, cloud and DevOps, cybersecurity, design and video. Start with an individual service or discuss a combination around your business goals. The proposed work, deliverables, responsibilities and costs are defined through an agreed scope. Our wider direction includes developing ARADUN-owned software and digital products; individual offerings will be described according to their actual readiness.

**Mission — D:** Help businesses solve practical problems through thoughtful design, maintainable technology, clearer operations and informed digital growth.

**Vision — D:** Build a global technology company whose services and products help businesses operate effectively and develop over time.

**Homepage value proposition — D:** Connect your digital presence, systems and workflows around a clear business goal.

**Headline recommendation — D:** Technology and digital services to build, automate, and grow your business.

**Alternative headlines — D, select one rather than rotate:** “Bring your digital plans into focus.” or “Build your next digital capability with a clear plan.” These are optional H1 alternatives, not replacements for the approved tagline. “Technology Without Boundaries.” remains an owner-provided supporting theme awaiting public-wording approval. The Arabic-origin story remains B until its linguistic wording and intended attribution are verified.

| Messaging pillar | What copy should demonstrate | Suitable proof |
|---|---|---|
| Business clarity | Explain the problem, user and proposed deliverable | Discovery questions, scoped proposal, annotated work |
| Connected capabilities | Explain useful relationships between services | Reviewed workflow map; actual integrated project |
| Maintainable delivery | Describe handoff, documentation and support boundaries | Permitted handoff artifact; actual delivery checklist |
| Responsible technology | Explain access, data and security considerations without guarantees | Authorized review process; verified implementation evidence |
| Working partnership | Explain responsibilities, reviews and change handling | Approved process and genuine attributed feedback |

These are positioning principles to substantiate, not claims that ARADUN outperforms competitors.

Voice: composed, helpful, specific and technically credible. Prefer active verbs and short paragraphs. Use “you” for the prospect and “we” for approved company commitments. Explain CRM, CI/CD and other unfamiliar acronyms at first meaningful use. Avoid “world-leading,” “revolutionary,” “guaranteed growth,” “100% secure,” “unlimited revisions,” “24/7 support” and generic superlatives.

Use consistent US-English spelling as an R9 editorial convention for the English edition; country-neutral meaning takes priority. Preserve proper names and exact catalog terminology, including Digital Marketing, AI Chatbot, Workflow Automation and Website Terms. “Quote request” means a request for review, not an issued quote. “Consultation request” means pending confirmation. “Draft saved” is not “Published.” “Won” is not “Paid.”

Headings communicate meaning rather than keywords alone. Use one descriptive H1, logical H2/H3 nesting, visible form labels, descriptive links and brief error recovery instructions. Suggested lengths are editing aids rather than mandatory quotas: card summaries about 15–30 words, introductory paragraphs about 40–80 words, and FAQ answers as short as the question allows. Preserve comprehension when translated later.

## 5. Company, overview and utility-page copy

All text below is D unless it is an unchanged approved label. Appendix A retains each exact section sequence, audience, route, metadata, CTA and requirements. This section supplies the prose and population rules for those regions.

### PUB-002 — Services

H1: “Find the right services for your business.” Intro: “Explore eight areas of technology and digital work. Choose a category to compare services, understand the possible scope and request a quote.”

Category directory uses §6. Help choosing: “Start with what you want to achieve: a clearer online presence, a new application, less repetitive work, stronger marketing, or improvements to your technology.” Engagement options: “Choose an individual service or discuss a combination. One-time, monthly, setup-plus-monthly and custom engagements depend on the selected offer.” Buyer questions cover selecting a category, combining services, maintenance and custom needs using §11. Closing: “Tell us what you need, even if you are still defining the project.” No unsupported all-services-available claim.

### PUB-003 — Solutions

H1: “Find a solution around your business goals.” Intro: “Explore five ways to connect relevant services around a business need. Each engagement starts with a review of your requirements and an agreed scope.” Render §8 cards. Selection guide: digital presence → SOL-01; repetitive administration → SOL-02; coordinated marketing → SOL-03; deployment/infrastructure → SOL-04; several connected needs → SOL-05. Approach: “Review the goal, identify suitable services, agree the work and costs, then define delivery and review points.” FAQs distinguish a solution from a fixed all-inclusive bundle. Closing: “Let’s identify the right approach.”

### PUB-004 — Pricing & Packages

H1: “Understand your options before you start.” Intro: “Explore indicative starting prices and engagement models. Your final quote reflects the work, responsibilities and costs agreed for your project.”

Four models: “One-time: a defined engagement with an agreed fee.” “Monthly: recurring work with agreed scope and billing terms.” “Setup plus monthly: implementation and ongoing work priced separately.” “Custom: a quote prepared after reviewing your requirements.” Show the exact five-solution table and link to authoritative service comparisons. Combined/custom region: “Select the services you want to discuss, or describe your needs. We’ll review overlap, dependencies and scope before preparing a quote.” Cost expectations reference §9; no blind summed price or invented inclusions. Closing: “Get a quote for your requirements.”

### PUB-005 — About ARADUN

1. Introduction: “Technology & Digital Solutions for businesses.” Use §3 extended description, shortened where needed; no claim of released ARADUN products.
2. Purpose and direction: use proposed mission/vision/values from §3 after approval. Values may explain innovation through practical choices, quality through review, security through appropriate controls, clarity through scope, partnership through responsibilities, and growth as an intended business benefit.
3. People: requires verified display name, role, approved biography, accurately attributed experience and photo permission. No team-size inference. Do not turn the founder’s personal experience into ARADUN’s years in business. Internal missing-content marker: `[REQUIRES VERIFIED PEOPLE CONTENT]`; never public text.
4. How we work: “We begin with your goals and constraints, define the scope, and agree how progress, reviews and changes will be handled. Handoff and any ongoing support are included in the engagement discussion.” This is D pending operational commitment review.
5. Evidence: actual permitted project cards and testimonials only; follow §10. No invented client logo strip.
6. Start a conversation: “Tell us about the work you are planning.” Get a Quote and Contact Us. Dhaka, Bangladesh is owner-supplied city information, not a verified registered-office address. Public phone, handling hours and legal entity require confirmation.

### PUB-006 — Our Work

H1: “Explore our work.” Intro for a populated collection: “Explore projects, their challenges and the work delivered. Each project is labeled to distinguish client work, ARADUN internal products and concept or demo work.” Filters remain conditional on useful collection size; no empty filter taxonomy invented. Project cards use §10. Empty copy when appropriate: “There are no published projects to show here yet. You can explore our services or contact us about your requirements.” A sparse page’s indexing/publication must be reviewed; do not invent evidence to fill it. Closing: “Planning a similar project? Tell us about your goals and constraints.”

### PUB-007 — Case Study / Project Detail

Use §10’s seven-region record contract. No case-study title, customer, numeric result or project instance is asserted by this document. Actual slugs and metadata are instance-specific and approved before publication. The reusable page remains fully in Phase 1.

### PUB-008 — Insights & Resources

H1: “Practical guidance for your digital decisions.” Intro: “Explore explanations, planning questions and practical resources for choosing and managing technology and digital services.” Topics use §11’s controlled dictionary; publish only populated relevant topics. Article cards show actual title, excerpt, topic, author/date and approved image. Empty copy: “No articles are published here yet. Explore our services or contact us with a question.” Closing: “Need help applying an idea to your business? Explore a related service or request a consultation.”

### PUB-009 — Article / Resource Detail

Use §11’s six-region editorial brief. Each publication needs a genuine accountable author, accurate dates, distinct topic, complete body, checked sources for material factual claims and reviewed metadata. No publication date, expertise credential or article instance is invented.

### PUB-010 — FAQs & Help

H1: “How can we help?” Intro: “Find answers about services, pricing, delivery and working with ARADUN.” Search label: “Search questions and answers.” Group answers by Services, Pricing, Delivery, Support and Working with ARADUN. Use §11’s draft answer bank, approving any operational commitments before publication. Closing: “Still need help? Send us your question.” Contact is the primary recovery action; Quote and Consultation are contextual alternatives.

### PUB-011 — Detailed Help Article

Use §11’s five-region help brief. Short FAQ answers remain anchored entries in `/faq`; only substantive distinct answers get a detail record. Do not duplicate an Insights body under a second route. Proposed help topics are not live records or additional top-level pages.

### PUB-012 — Contact

H1: “How can we help?” Intro: “Send a general question, partnership inquiry or message about working with ARADUN. For project scope and pricing, you can also request a quote.” Required fields: Name, Email, Subject, Message. Submit label: “Send Message.” Contact details: `hello@aradun.com` after operational verification; city and social links only as approved. Next-step text: “We’ll review your message and route it to the appropriate next step.” No response-time guarantee. Full state and consent copy: §12.

### PUB-013 — Get a Quote

H1: “Tell us about your project.” Intro: “Choose the services or solution you want to discuss, or describe your needs if you are unsure. We’ll review your request before preparing a quote.” Preserve Service → Project → Contact → Review. No login, payment or mandatory phone/company/budget. Pricing reminder: “This is a quote request. Displayed starting prices are indicative and do not confirm the final scope or fee.” §12 supplies step and state copy. Noindex; exclude from sitemap.

### PUB-014 — Consultation

H1: “Request an initial consultation.” Intro: “Use a free 15-minute initial screening to discuss your needs and a suitable next step.” Explain: “This is an initial discussion. Detailed discovery, audits or consulting work may need a separate agreed scope and fee.” No paid-discovery price is invented. Collect name, email, topic and preferred time/timezone in manual mode. Submit: “Request a Consultation.” Notice: “Your preferred time is a request. We’ll confirm arrangements separately.” Live availability/booking wording is conditional on D-003; no automatic promotion to Phase 1.

### PUB-015 — Inquiry Confirmation

Saved-state title: “Your request has been received.” Body: “Reference: [actual saved reference]. We’ll review your request and contact you about the next step.” Consultation variant adds “Your meeting time is not confirmed yet.” Email delay variant: “Your request is saved. A confirmation email may arrive later; you do not need to submit it again.” Only show delivery-specific statements supported by actual state. Direct visit: “Use Contact, Get a Quote or Consultation to send a request.” Do not display a fake reference or expose records through reference lookup. Noindex; exclude from sitemap and conversion counting based only on page view.

### PUB-016 — Search Results

H1: “Search ARADUN.” Label: “Search services, solutions, articles and help.” Result types and snippets come from published eligible content. No-match: “No results found for ‘[safely rendered query]’. Try a different term or explore our services.” Failure: “Search is unavailable right now. Try again or browse our services.” These are different states. Clear search and pagination labels remain usable by keyboard. Do not imply that portfolio, industry or product search is already included. Noindex; exclude from sitemap.

### PUB-017–018 and TRU-01–03 — Policy pages

Exact page names: Privacy Policy, Website Terms, Cookie Information, Accessibility Statement, Responsible Disclosure. §20 defines their required content and factual/legal gates. A page’s planned heading is not a completed legal policy. All use T-POLICY without sales-pressure CTAs; the three TRU entries remain conditional.

## 6. Eight service-category content specifications

Each category uses T-CATEGORY’s seven sections: hero; assigned services; selection guide; evidence; engagement process; FAQs; next step. The following category records combine with the exact membership/URL register in Appendix A and service copy in §7. All proposed narrative is D; service readiness, project evidence and package scope are B. Category cards must link all assigned services, showing only truthful availability and approved price cues. The selection guidance is editorial, not an automatic bundle.

### CAT-01 — Web & Software Development

**URL:** `/services/web-software-development` · **Status:** D/B/V · **Primary intent:** commercial comparison.

**H1:** Web & Software Development. **Supporting headline:** Build a clearer digital presence and useful business software.

**Intro / value:** Explore web & software development around a defined business goal. Build a clearer digital presence and useful business software. The selected work, inputs, costs and responsibilities are agreed before delivery.

**Ideal clients:** Business owners and product teams planning a website, online store or custom workflow. **Typical problem:** An unclear website, disconnected operations, or a system that no longer fits.

**Service overview / selection guide:** Use Business Website Development for a company/service website; E-commerce Website Development for a store; Custom Web Application for bespoke workflows; Website Redesign for an existing-site change; Website Maintenance for agreed recurring upkeep.

**Exact membership:** SVC-001 Business Website Development, SVC-002 E-commerce Website Development, SVC-003 Custom Web Application, SVC-004 Website Redesign, SVC-005 Website Maintenance.

**Benefits to describe:** clearer choices and responsibilities, a scope matched to the problem, and reviewable deliverables. These are intended benefits, not verified results. **Process:** review goals and existing systems; agree structure and scope; design/build; verify key journeys; hand off.

**Evidence/assets:** actual permitted examples relevant to this category; `CAT-ART-01` and neutral service icons; original diagram or labeled concept only where useful. No invented client outcomes. **Related services:** SVC-043; SVC-018; SVC-028. **Related solutions:** Digital Presence; Complete Digital Business; informational relationships only.

**Category FAQs — D:**

1. **Do I need a website or a custom application?** A content-led business presence and a workflow application solve different needs. Start with users and tasks, then compare SVC-001 and SVC-003.
2. **Can you maintain an existing website?** Existing-site support depends on its technology, access and condition. Assessment and the agreed maintenance boundary determine what can be covered.
3. **How do I choose a starting point?** Use Business Website Development for a company/service website; E-commerce Website Development for a store; Custom Web Application for bespoke workflows; Website Redesign for an existing-site change; Website Maintenance for agreed recurring upkeep.
4. **What should I prepare?** Share your goal, existing setup, constraints and available materials. The selected service page identifies more specific inputs; sensitive access is exchanged only through an agreed secure process.

**CTAs:** Get a Quote → `/get-a-quote`; Request a Consultation → `/consultation`; exact service and solution routes. Preserve category context without treating it as a purchased package.

**SEO title draft:** Web & Software Development | ARADUN. **Description draft:** Build a clearer digital presence and useful business software. Explore ARADUN’s services, scope considerations and quote options. **Keyword themes:** website and software services; business software development. See Appendix A for the canonical and exact section order.

### CAT-02 — Mobile App Development

**URL:** `/services/mobile-app-development` · **Status:** D/B/V · **Primary intent:** commercial comparison.

**H1:** Mobile App Development. **Supporting headline:** Plan, design and develop mobile experiences around real tasks.

## 7. All 57 individual service content specifications

**Common contract:** Every record below instantiates T-SERVICE’s exact nine-section recipe in Appendix B: hero, fit/outcomes, scope, evidence, commercial options, delivery, related offers, FAQs and final CTA. H1 is the exact approved service name; the supporting line can express its business need. Each record has a distinct summary, problem, potential deliverables, benefit, process, input requirements, distinguishing FAQ, visuals, related links and metadata. Appendix A supplies exact URL, audience, source section sequence and FR traceability.

**Scope honesty:** Deliverables below are candidate scope topics for discussion, not approved Starter/Professional inclusions. The specific proposal/package must confirm quantities, exclusions, revisions, timing, ownership, accessibility obligations, technology choice, licensing and maintenance. Do not convert the candidate list into package checkmarks. Exact source prices are preserved per record and in Appendix C. No ambiguous period is guessed. Delivery availability must be approved per service before its public offer is enabled.

**Copy state:** All newly drafted summaries, benefits, approaches and FAQ answers are D; capability, permission and package evidence are B. The proposed scope is not an assurance that every specialty is available in-house. Security services require authorized scope and qualified capability. AI copy must explain review/failure handling without promising autonomous accuracy. Marketing copy must not guarantee rankings, leads or revenue.

**Common FAQs supplementing each distinguishing answer:** Each service receives four complete proposed answers: its source-specific distinguishing question, service-specific inputs, inclusions/fees and timing/support. Further unique questions may be added when substantiated; do not repeat generic FAQ bodies solely for SEO. Retainers need explicit workload/cadence/overage/cancellation details before final terms are public.

## 8. Five solution content specifications

All five use T-SOLUTION’s nine sections: hero, fit, scope relationships, packages, delivery, evidence, related options, FAQs and final CTA. Candidate service relationships below explain plausible coordination only. The source `Solution.serviceIds` can remain empty while contractual composition is unresolved; these recommendations do not populate approved package inclusions. Every solution retains Starter, Professional and Enterprise, with exact source prices in §9/Appendix C.

### SOL-001 / SOL-01 — Digital Presence

**URL:** `/solutions/digital-presence` · **Status:** D/B/V · **H1:** Digital Presence. **Headline/support:** Build a clear starting point for your digital presence.

**Description:** An unclear website, inconsistent identity or missing inquiry path. Build a clear starting point for your digital presence. This solution offers a route to discuss connected work, with the final services, deliverables and costs defined in the proposal.

**Target customer:** New businesses or organizations improving how they explain and present their offer. **Problems addressed:** An unclear website, inconsistent identity or missing inquiry path. **Intended benefits:** clearer priorities, better-defined responsibilities and related work considered together; no guaranteed business result.

**Candidate category relationships:** Web & Software Development; UI/UX, Graphic Design & Branding; relevant Digital Marketing. **Related service candidates:** SVC-001, SVC-004, SVC-043, SVC-045, SVC-047. These are informational recommendations, not approved included-service lists. Quantities, exclusions, revisions, client inputs, technology, setup/recurring costs and support remain package decisions. See §9 and Appendix C for exact prices and original qualifications.

**Delivery approach:** review audience and offer; agree identity/content/site scope; design/build selected deliverables; verify inquiry paths; hand off. Explain client inputs, access, reviews, acceptance and handoff. Confirm timelines and any ongoing services separately.

**Evidence/assets:** actual relevant work or labeled concept, `SOL-ART-01` showing the specific relationship among selected capabilities; no invented client stories or outcome numbers. **Related solutions:** SOL-03, SOL-05; links should help buyers choose a narrower or alternative scope.

**FAQs — D:**

1. **Does this include every branding and website service?** No. The final package identifies included services and deliverables. The solution name and starting price do not establish a complete branding or website scope.
2. **Can this work with our existing systems?** Existing systems are reviewed for access, compatibility, licensing and condition. Reuse or replacement must be justified and agreed; neither is automatic.
3. **What determines the final scope and fee?** Your objectives, the selected services (SVC-001, SVC-004, SVC-043, SVC-045, SVC-047), deliverable quantities, dependencies and ongoing responsibilities determine the proposal. Starting prices are indicative and third-party costs must be identified.
4. **How is the engagement delivered?** We propose to review audience and offer; agree identity/content/site scope; design/build selected deliverables; verify inquiry paths; hand off. The actual sequence, review points and milestones are agreed before delivery.

**CTA:** Get a Quote retaining solution/tier context; Request a Consultation for clarification. No public checkout or binding automatic bundle total.

**SEO title draft:** Digital Presence | ARADUN. **Description draft:** Build a clear starting point for your digital presence. Explore ARADUN’s Digital Presence solution, engagement options and scope considerations. **Keyword themes:** digital presence services; business website and branding. Intent: coordinated-solution commercial evaluation.

## 9. Packages, pricing and commercial content

One maintained catalog owns names, prices, units and scope. Prices are indicative USD starting points; no automatic contractual quote or public checkout. Existing proposed price increases in D-002 remain unapproved and are not applied. Two-price service entries retain Starter/Professional. Three-price recurring entries retain source order without inventing tier names. “Custom” is a reviewed scope, not USD 0 or an undisclosed substitute for a known source price. Service and solution IDs use different namespaces from page IDs.

| ID | Solution | Starter | Professional | Enterprise |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SOL-001 | Digital Presence | From $899/project | From $2,499/project | Custom |
| SOL-002 | Business Automation | From $699/project | From $2,499/project | Custom |
| SOL-003 | Business Growth | From $399/month | From $999/month | Custom |
| SOL-004 | Cloud & Technology | From $499/project | From $1,999/project | Custom |
| SOL-005 | Complete Digital Business | Custom | Custom | Custom |

Names/prices are confirmed concepts from S1 §5. Exact included services, quantities, exclusions, revisions, timelines, setup fees, recurring costs, and upgrade/maintenance rules are not fully specified in the supplied sources. Do not infer a bundle's contractual scope from its name. Each solution must have one maintained package definition and enter the unified quote flow. Existing canonical solution URLs in S1 remain reference constraints; final page inventory belongs to Step 2.

For **every service package and each of the 15 solution tier positions**, the readiness checklist is: exact tier label/source ordinal; intended fit; deliverables and measurable limits; client inputs; exclusions; revision policy; timing dependencies; setup fee if applicable; recurring fee and unit; third-party spend/licenses; applicable tax treatment; production/travel costs where relevant; ownership/license terms; handoff; maintenance/support scope; availability; scope version and approval evidence. Unknown values stay internally pending. Do not publish blank cells, invented “unlimited” features or a purchasable scope assembled from a name alone.

Public draft disclaimer: “Starting prices are indicative. Final scope, fees and any ongoing or third-party costs are confirmed in your proposal.” For recurring services: “The monthly scope, billing terms and any usage limits are agreed before work begins.” For custom work: “Request a quote so we can review your requirements.” For setup-plus-monthly offers, present setup and recurring amounts separately only when both are confirmed.

Package selection carries the approved service/solution/package identity into the unified quote flow. A custom/unsure route remains available. Selecting related services does not compel purchase. Deduplicate SVC-009 across its two categories. Review overlapping deliverables rather than blindly summing all public starting prices. Never add one-time and monthly amounts into a single total.

**Existing payment planning guidance, preserved with qualification:** Starter project 50% deposit/50% before final handoff; Professional 40%/30%/30% at agreed milestones; Enterprise custom milestones; retainers monthly in advance, generally 30-day cancellation notice subject to signed terms and no unused-hour rollover unless agreed. These are planning policies, not finalized universal legal terms. Fees for detailed discovery, actual providers, eligibility, cancellation, refunds, taxes and jurisdiction-specific terms remain decisions. Won does not mean Paid. Phase 1 collection uses external proposals/agreements/invoices; no payment gateway, signing platform or invoice engine is implied.

<a id="s9-10"></a>

## 10. Portfolio, case studies and testimonials

**Portfolio source model:** use approved `CaseStudy` source records, not a new MongoDB collection. Required planning fields: stable contentKey, actual slug/title, classification, problem, contribution, approach, outcome, permitted assets, related service IDs, publication, SEO and version. Evidence remains private. See S5 §8.1 for authoritative fields.

| T-CASE region | Required information | Evidence/publication check |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Introduction | Actual title, classification, concise summary, permitted representative image | Permission for client identity/brand; concept classification visible |
| 2. Problem and constraints | Actual situation, users, goals and material limitations | No invented business baseline or confidential disclosure |
| 3. Scope and contribution | Work performed by ARADUN or accurately attributed individual; collaborators and exclusions | Do not claim a previous employer’s contract as ARADUN’s |
| 4. Approach | Decisions, reasons, relevant technologies and trade-offs | Describe actual implementation, not every technology in the catalog |
| 5. Delivered work | Screens/artifacts with captions and relevant detail | Redact PII, secrets, customer records and restricted assets |
| 6. Outcomes | Delivered capability; metrics only if supportable with method, period and attribution limits | No invented percentage uplift; permission and source recorded privately |
| 7. Next step | Related published service and similar-need quote CTA | Relationship must be genuine; no compulsory bundle |

Project card copy pattern: `[Actual project title]` → `[Client Work / ARADUN Internal Product / Concept/Demo]` → `[one factual sentence about problem and contribution]` → “View Project.” This is a schema/example, not publishable filler. Actual detail SEO: `[Approved project title] | ARADUN`; description identifies actual work and outcome without unsubstantiated metrics.

If client work cannot be disclosed, seek permission for an anonymized account with truthful limitations, use actual ARADUN internal work, or propose a clearly labeled concept/demo. Do not create a fake client story, build an unrelated AMS just to fill the portfolio, or treat anonymization as automatic permission. No minimum invented project count is required.

**Testimonial workflow:** genuine quote; permitted display name and optional role/company/avatar; private source and permission; optional real case relation; related offer IDs; explicit state private/published/hidden; accountable editor and publication. Preserve quotation meaning. Permission withdrawal hides the record promptly. Editing a published quotation/attribution follows the source’s hide/re-review behavior. `isDemo` fictional quotations cannot publish in production. No public review submission, star-rating aggregation or self-created award is added.

<a id="s9-11"></a>

## 11. Blog, FAQ and knowledge-base content

One Insights hub contains articles/resources. FAQs & Help contains short answers and distinct substantive help pages. No new `/blog`, `/knowledge-base`, category archive or downloadable-resource route is introduced. Resource files attach to useful content; no new email gate is required.

**Article brief using T-ARTICLE:** (1) actual title, excerpt, accountable author, real dates/topic and optional feature image; (2) immediate takeaway and contents only when useful; (3) distinct main body with examples, sources and accessible visual explanation; (4) optional practical checklist/resource; (5) related published reading and services; (6) relevant next step. Draft one focused question per article. Verify time-sensitive technical claims against current primary documentation when writing that article. No search-volume or ranking claim is made here.

**Detailed-help brief using T-HELP:** question and immediate answer; when it applies; complete explanation/steps; related answers/offers; further-help contact. Example proposed topic “What to include in a website project brief” belongs in one chosen content namespace, not duplicated in Insights and Help.

Proposed topic dictionary, D: Websites & Software; Mobile Applications; Automation & CRM; Digital Marketing; Cloud & DevOps; Cybersecurity; Design & Branding; Video; Project Planning. Topic keys are controlled source configuration, not new collections or route families. FAQ groups follow the five source groups: Services, Pricing, Delivery, Support, Working with ARADUN.

| Draft ID | Proposed editorial title | Audience / intent | Outline and related offer | Asset / review need |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ED-01 | Business website or custom web application: which fits your need? | Owners comparing options | Users/tasks → content site vs workflow tool → scope questions; SVC-001/003 | Exact comparison diagram; no invented costs |
| ED-02 | What to include in a useful project brief | Prospects preparing an inquiry | Goals → users → existing system → constraints → references; quote flow | Reusable checklist; no mandatory download gate |
| ED-03 | Which workflows are worth automating first? | Operations leads | Frequency → rules → exceptions → data/access → review; SVC-011 | Synthetic labeled workflow example |
| ED-04 | Preparing customer data for a CRM migration | Sales/operations | Fields → duplicates → permissions → mapping → verification; SVC-012 | Synthetic data only; privacy review |
| ED-05 | Understanding setup, project and ongoing digital costs | Buyers comparing proposals | Fee models → third-party spend → scope → maintenance; Pricing | No invented package commitments |
| ED-06 | Planning a business mobile app before development | Founders | User tasks → platform needs → design → integrations → release; SVC-006/009 | Labeled prototype; store-policy facts checked when drafted |
| ED-07 | What an SEO engagement can and cannot promise | Marketing buyers | Audit → priorities → implementation → measurement → uncertainty; SVC-018 | Current primary search guidance; no ranking promise |
| ED-08 | Questions to ask before moving an application to cloud hosting | Technical buyers | Workload → access → data → deployment → monitoring → recovery; SVC-027/028 | Architecture diagram without sensitive infrastructure |
| ED-09 | Preparing for an authorized web security assessment | Technical owners | Written scope → access → safe testing → findings → follow-up; SVC-035 | Qualified reviewer; no exploit instructions or certification claim |
| ED-10 | A brand handoff checklist for digital projects | Founders/marketing | Logo variants → type/color → assets → rights → usage; SVC-045 | Licensed example assets |
| ED-11 | Preparing footage for a video editing project | Marketing teams | Goal → footage → audio → rights → references → output; SVC-050/051 | Licensed or original sample footage |
| ED-12 | What to agree before a maintenance engagement starts | Existing-site/app owners | Supported systems → workload → access → exclusions → escalation; SVC-005/010/017 | Operational terms approved before advice becomes company promise |

These are proposed topics, not approved publishable articles, research findings, allocated live slugs or a mandatory launch quantity. Prioritize ED-01–05 according to available expertise and buyer questions. Owner/editor selects actual authors and a manageable cadence. R9 recommendation: review the backlog monthly and reassess evergreen content quarterly or when facts change; this is an internal workflow suggestion, not a public publishing promise. Publish only complete, reviewed work. Draft → protected preview → authorized publish/unpublish; no scheduled publication or full historical revision UI is added.

**Draft common FAQ answer bank:**

| ID / question | Proposed answer | Dependencies |
|---|---|---|
| FAQ-01 Can I request one service or combine several? | You can request an individual service or discuss a combination. We review the scope and any overlapping work before preparing a quote. | Catalog/quote policy |
| FAQ-02 What if I do not know which service I need? | Choose Custom/Not sure in the quote form, describe your goal, or request an initial consultation. | Preserve actual selected control labels |
| FAQ-03 Are the displayed prices final? | They are indicative starting prices. Your proposal confirms the agreed scope, fees and any separate ongoing or third-party costs. | Billing units and package details must be approved |
| FAQ-04 How long will my project take? | Timing depends on scope, complexity, access, content and review availability. Delivery milestones are agreed for the engagement. | No invented duration |
| FAQ-05 Can we work together from another country? | ARADUN’s services are intended for international businesses. Communication, scheduling, payment arrangements and any location-specific constraints are confirmed for each engagement. | Actual supported arrangements B; no worldwide-office claim |
| FAQ-06 Is maintenance included? | Maintenance and ongoing support are included only when stated in the agreed scope. Separate maintenance options can be discussed where available. | Exact support terms B |
| FAQ-07 What happens after I send a quote request? | Your request is saved for review. We may ask for clarification before discussing scope and preparing a proposal. | No automatic quote or response deadline |
| FAQ-08 Is the first consultation free? | The initial screening is free and lasts 15 minutes. Detailed discovery or specialist work may need a separately agreed paid engagement. | No invented paid tier price |
| FAQ-09 Does a consultation request confirm a meeting? | No. Under the current request process, your preferred time is pending confirmation. | D-003 manual baseline |
| FAQ-10 Do you include hosting, advertising or software subscriptions? | These costs are separate unless your proposal explicitly includes them. The proposal identifies the relevant third-party and ongoing costs. | Per-offer exceptions must be explicit |
| FAQ-11 Can I change the scope after work begins? | Proposed changes are reviewed for their effect on deliverables, fees and timing before additional work is agreed. | Change-control commitment review |
| FAQ-12 Who owns the completed work? | Ownership, licenses, source files and third-party restrictions are defined in the agreement for your engagement. | Legal/commercial approval; no blanket IP transfer promise |
| FAQ-13 Can you work with an existing website or system? | Share the current setup and the changes you need. Feasibility depends on access, licensing, technical condition and the agreed scope. | Service-specific capability review |
| FAQ-14 How do payments work? | Fees and milestones are documented in your proposal and agreement. Phase 1 payment collection is handled through the agreed external invoice/payment process. | Provider/eligibility/terms B; do not name an unselected provider |
| FAQ-15 Do you guarantee rankings, sales or security? | Outcomes depend on factors beyond an individual engagement. The proposal defines the work and how it will be assessed; no blanket ranking, revenue or security guarantee is offered. | Preserve truthful service descriptions |

FAQ-01–06 populate the homepage preview. Offer-specific FAQs in §§6–8 add real distinctions. Reuse maintained answers where meaning is identical; avoid contradictory copies. Full support/refund/cancellation answers remain pending §19/20 rather than invented.

## 12. Conversion forms and transactional microcopy

Use visible labels, optional markers and persistent instructions. Marketing opt-in is separate from any legally appropriate privacy acknowledgement and is never preselected. Do not require a business email domain; valid personal email is allowed. No public attachments, account registration or checkout is added.

| Flow / region | Labels and proposed copy | State/behavior requirement |
|---|---|---|
| Quote / Service | “What would you like help with?”; Services, Solutions, Custom/Not sure; selected items; “Change selection” | Keep exact catalog names/IDs; multi-selection deduplicated; package context editable |
| Quote / Project | “Tell us about your project”; Project brief; Budget (optional); Preferred timeline (optional); Company/Website where source allocates optional fields | Do not promise budget fit or selected start date |
| Quote / Contact | Name; Email; optional approved company/website fields; privacy notice | Do not collect extra sensitive data |
| Quote / Review | “Review your request”; Service, Project, Contact summaries; Edit; “Submit Quote Request” | Show prices as indicative and separate units; no invented total |
| Step controls | Back; Continue; “Step [n] of 4: [Service/Project/Contact/Review]” | Announce new step and manage focus; retain valid input |
| Contact | Name; Email; Subject; Message; “Send Message” | Required fields exactly as S3; optional context transparent |
| Consultation | Name; Email; Meeting topic; Preferred time; Timezone; “Request a Consultation” | Manual baseline; timezone-aware, no fake availability |
| Coming-soon offer | Availability label “Coming soon”; “Ask about this service” where approved as availability-appropriate inquiry | Do not promise delivery/start date; no waitlist feature inferred |
| Specialist-led offer | Availability label “Specialist-led”; explain actual delivery arrangement only after approval | No invented specialist identity/certification |
| Blank required field | “Enter your [field label].” | Associate error to field; clear error summary |
| Invalid email | “Enter a valid email address, such as name@example.com.” | Example only; no domain restriction |
| Missing service/custom choice | “Choose a service or solution, or select Custom/Not sure.” | Allow custom path without fabricated offer |
| Missing timezone | “Choose your timezone so we can understand your preferred time.” | No silent ambiguous timezone conversion |
| Oversized input | “Shorten this field to [actual enforced limit] characters.” | Use validator’s real limit, not a guessed number |
| Submitting | “Sending your request…” | Disable duplicate submit only while appropriate; static reduced-motion status |
| Saved | “Your request has been received. Reference: [actual reference].” | Only after durable save; no public record lookup |
| Saved, mail delayed | “Your request is saved. A confirmation email may arrive later; you do not need to submit it again.” | Use only if supported by delivery state |
| Not saved | “We couldn’t save your request. Your entries are still here. Please try again.” | Use only when failure is known; preserve valid input |
| Outcome unknown | “We couldn’t confirm the result yet. Use the retry option to check or complete this request.” | Retry same idempotency context; do not invite duplicate submissions |
| Rate limit | “Please wait before trying again.” | Add exact wait only when backend supplies it; accessible recovery |
| Offer changed | “Offer information has changed. Review the updated details before submitting.” | Preserve answers and show actual changed context |
| Network issue | “Connection interrupted. Check your connection and try again.” | Distinguish from a known saved result |
| Manual consultation saved | “Your consultation request is received. Your meeting time is pending confirmation.” | Never “Your meeting is booked” under manual baseline |

**Proposed privacy helper — D/L:** “Read our Privacy Policy to understand how your information is handled.” If the selected lawful workflow requires acknowledgement, proposed label: “I have read the Privacy Policy.” Do not equate acknowledgement with blanket consent or marketing opt-in. Exact wording follows D-014 and actual practices. Optional marketing wording, if an approved subscription use exists: “I would like to receive [accurately described communications].” Do not enable a campaign/newsletter feature merely because this wording exists.

**Customer email drafts — D, sent only from verified identity after saved state:**

- Quote subject: “We received your ARADUN quote request — [reference]”. Body: “Hello [submitted name], we received your quote request. Your reference is [reference]. We’ll review your requirements and contact you about the next step. This acknowledgement is not a quote or agreement. ARADUN — Technology & Digital Solutions.”
- Contact subject: “We received your message — [reference]”. Body: “Hello [submitted name], thank you for contacting ARADUN. Your message has been received for review. Reference: [reference].”
- Consultation subject: “Your ARADUN consultation request — [reference]”. Body: “Hello [submitted name], we received your request for a free 15-minute initial screening. Your preferred time is pending confirmation. We’ll contact you about arrangements. Reference: [reference].”
- Staff alert: “New [quote/contact/consultation] inquiry — [reference]”. Include minimal routing context and a protected staff link. Escape user content, omit unnecessary sensitive details and never expose internal staff links in customer messages. No actual messages are sent by this planning task.

Use current saved facts and allowlisted templates; no raw HTML from user fields. A mail provider’s acceptance is not proof of delivery. Confirmation copy and analytics must match actual state. No response SLA, booking guarantee, proposal deadline or automatic customer conversion is invented.

## 13. Navigation and interface wording

The following public navigation contract is preserved from S2 §5. Historical references to later Step 4 are resolved by S4; source labels and destinations remain unchanged.

### 5.1 Desktop header

| Order / element | Label | Destination / behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Brand | ARADUN logo | Home `/` |
| 1 | Services | `/services`; accessible category menu |
| 2 | Solutions | `/solutions`; five-solution menu |
| 3 | Pricing | `/pricing` |
| 4 | Our Work | `/our-work` |
| 5 | About | `/about` |
| 6 | Insights | `/insights` |
| 7 | Contact | `/contact`; visible direct desktop link |
| Utility | Search | Accessible search control leading to `/search?q=…` |
| Primary CTA | Get a Quote | `/get-a-quote` |

The visible Contact link carries forward the final refinement in this chat. It remains distinct from Get a Quote: general/client/partnership questions use Contact; project scope and pricing requests use the quote flow. At widths where the full header cannot fit accessibly, switch to the responsive menu rather than remove Contact or shrink targets excessively. Exact breakpoint and presentation belong to Step 4.

### 5.2 Services menu

All Services → `/services`, followed by the eight CAT-01–CAT-08 destinations in their catalog order. Use grouped labels and optional selected service links without putting all 57 services into the top-level header. Featured links must target published service pages. A category remains a usable destination even when some services have no published detail yet. Category/service names remain source-consistent; shortened visual labels require content review, not identity changes.

### 5.3 Solutions menu

All Solutions → `/solutions`; Digital Presence → `/solutions/digital-presence`; Business Automation → `/solutions/business-automation`; Business Growth → `/solutions/business-growth`; Cloud & Technology → `/solutions/cloud-technology`; Complete Digital Business → `/solutions/complete-digital-business`.

### 5.4 Mobile navigation

Expose Home, Services, Solutions, Pricing, Our Work, About, Insights, Contact, Consultation, search and Get a Quote. Services/Solutions expand to the same published destinations as desktop. Provide explicit All Services/All Solutions links. Closing, back navigation, Escape where applicable, focus restoration, readable labels and keyboard operation must work; the menu cannot depend on hover. Retain an easy-to-find Contact link and quote action. Do not add staff navigation to the public menu.

### 5.5 Footer

| Group | Destinations |
|---|---|
| Services | Services overview and CAT-01–CAT-08 |
| Solutions | Solutions overview and SOL-01–SOL-05 |
| Company | About, Our Work, Contact Us |
| Resources | Insights & Resources, FAQs & Help |
| Start a Project | Pricing, Get a Quote, Consultation |
| Legal | Privacy Policy, Website Terms; conditional trust policies only when approved and published |
| Contact and social | `hello@aradun.com` after mailbox activation; approved LinkedIn, Facebook, YouTube, X and Instagram links from the master; only verified/publish-approved phone information |

Do not invent GitHub, sales/support mailboxes, street addresses or offices. The master records Dhaka, Bangladesh, and selected but not yet operational `hello@aradun.com`. There is no public Staff Login link. A future Client Portal utility/footer link may appear only after the P2 portal is operational and approved.

### 5.6 Breadcrumbs

Breadcrumb allocation is a consolidation recommendation supporting the inventory hierarchy. Use Home → Services → Category → Service, Home → Solutions → Solution, Home → Our Work → Project, Home → Insights → Article, and Home → FAQs & Help → Detailed Answer. Conditional industry/product pages follow their hubs. Home itself needs no breadcrumb. Single-task quote/confirmation/auth flows need no elaborate breadcrumb trail; a clear exit/back action is sufficient. Staff/client breadcrumbs remain within their authorized application and never reveal inaccessible record names.

Flat service URLs still have a category breadcrumb. Cross-listed SVC-009 consistently uses CAT-02 as the canonical breadcrumb parent; changing the route based on discovery category is unnecessary. Related offers appear as related links, not invented hierarchy levels.

### 5.7 Contextual linking and conversion

| Starting point | Useful destination | Context retained / rule |
|---|---|---|
| Home | Categories, solutions, real work and quote | Clear company explanation and relevant next action |
| Category | Published service, relevant solution, quote | Correct service identity and availability |
| Service/package | Quote | Service/package IDs; server validates price and availability; snapshot preserves displayed commercial context |
| Solution/package | Quote | Solution/tier IDs; no blind sum or double charging of overlapping deliverables |
| Price comparison | Authoritative service/solution detail, then quote | One catalog source; indicative From prices, USD and explicit billing period |
| Case study | Related offers and quote | Truthful relationship; demo/client/internal label remains visible |
| Article/resource | Relevant service, solution, FAQ or work | Published references only; educational content is not duplicated across hubs |
| FAQ/help | Relevant offer, Contact or consultation | Short path when answer is insufficient |
| Contact | Consultation or quote where appropriate | Visitor can still submit a short general inquiry |
| Consultation | Pending-request confirmation | Timezone if preferences collected; no claim of booked slot before provider confirmation |
| Product | Information/demo inquiry through shared lead system | Actual product ID/status and available action only |
| Future industry | Existing service/solution/work | No duplicate service identities or unsupported sector credentials |
| Successful inquiry | Confirmation and relevant return links | Durable save first; generic safe fallback on direct confirmation visit |

Search indexes published services, solutions, articles and FAQs. Searching work, products or industries is not silently added to the confirmed P1 scope; related links may expose them, and search expansion requires approval. A category-only service entry can be a search target anchored within its published category, provided this is a useful public result and not a link to an unpublished detail. No drafts, internal leads, private portal data or staff notes enter public search.

Step 4 adds the Appearance utility without a new route: labels “Terracotta” and “Slate Blue,” with a selected state and accessible name. Search, Contact and Get a Quote remain usable at narrow widths. Use one outline icon family with neutral service icons; reserve semantic status colors for meaning and restrained brand accents for appropriate controls. No extra nav item for Packages, Team, Reviews, Technologies, countries or client portal in Phase 1.

| Context | Proposed label / message | Guard |
|---|---|---|
| Menu | Open menu; Close menu; All Services; All Solutions | Keyboard control, Escape/focus restoration, no hover-only discovery |
| Breadcrumb | Home → Services → [Category] → [Service] | Flat service URL stays canonical; SVC-009 parent CAT-02 |
| Search loading | Searching… | Do not show failed search as zero results |
| Result link | Actual result title, type and truthful snippet | Only eligible published content |
| Pagination | Previous; Next; Page [actual number] | Do not announce an invented total |
| Collection empty | No published [content type] yet | Hide irrelevant filters; do not synthesize records |
| Filter empty | No matches for these filters. Clear filters to try again. | Distinguish from no records |
| Loading | Loading [content/record type]… | Reserve layout; no essential information only in spinner |
| Safe page error | We couldn’t load this page. Try again or contact ARADUN. | No stack trace, provider diagnostic or PII |
| Not found | We couldn’t find that page. Search ARADUN or return Home. | True not-found response; no dedicated `/404` route |
| Save draft | Save Draft; Draft saved | Does not imply publication |
| Preview | Preview; Private preview — not published | Staff authorization; not a public token link |
| Publish | Publish; Published | Only after validation and authorized success |
| Unpublish | Unpublish; Removed from public view | Explain target and effect before consequential action |
| Edit conflict | This record changed since you opened it. Review the latest version before saving. | Preserve safe local edits; no silent overwrite |
| Unsaved navigation | You have unsaved changes. Leave without saving? | No autosave claim |
| Permission loss | You no longer have permission for this action. | Safe recovery; do not reveal inaccessible record details |
| Session expiry | Your session has expired. Sign in again to continue. | Safe return path; do not leak private state |
| Access denied | You do not have access to this page. | Distinguish only where safe; server enforcement mandatory |
| Authentication failure | We couldn’t sign you in with those details. | Provider-specific behavior; avoid account enumeration |
| Recovery request | If an eligible account exists, follow the recovery instructions sent through the configured process. | Use actual provider channel/policy once selected |
| Follow-up task | Due; Overdue; Completed; Cancelled | Current state, accessible text, no color-only meanings |
| Commercial pipeline | New → Qualified → Discovery → Proposal Sent → Negotiation → Won / Lost | Exact fixed source stages; not old simplified memory stages |
| Data distinctions | Estimated opportunity value; Invoiced amount; Received amount | Do not sum mixed currencies or label Won as Paid |

Appendix D binds all 31 staff/auth entries and two system states to their exact source routes and section sequences. Content labels do not expand role permissions or activate conditional screens. The public footer has no Staff Login link; the future Client Portal link remains deferred.

<a id="s9-14"></a>

## 14. SEO metadata, search intent and internal linking

Appendix A provides one record for each public page/template: primary/secondary keyword themes, draft title/description, H1 rule, canonical/indexing, schema opportunity and internal-link requirements. Themes are editorial recommendations, not researched volume, competition or ranking claims. Do not invent market demand figures.

Titles and descriptions must be unique, accurate and useful; exact approved service names remain intact even when long. Editorial targets of roughly 45–65 title characters and 120–160 description characters are R9 writing aids, not schema validation limits or guaranteed display lengths. S5 `SeoFields` safety bounds remain title ≤200 and description ≤500. Do not truncate meaningful names solely to meet an editorial target. Metadata for reusable detail templates is resolved per real content instance, not a literal `[slug]` page. One H1; ordered H2/H3 hierarchy; headings and alt text avoid keyword stuffing.

Canonical uses the reviewed `https://aradun.com` origin and exact source path after deployment configuration is verified. Tracking-only copies resolve to the clean page. No canonical from arbitrary Host headers. Keep SVC-009 one canonical despite cross-listing. Do not canonicalize all services to a category or all pagination to page one. Filters, selected-offer query strings and search must not create indexable duplicate pages. An approved slug change requires redirect, internal-link, sitemap and canonical migration; published slugs are locked by default.

Index only substantive eligible published content. `/get-a-quote`, `/thank-you`, `/search`, all staff/auth pages and protected previews are noindex and excluded from XML sitemap. Missing or unpublished detail records return safe not-found behavior. The three conditional policies enter navigation/sitemap only after approval and publication. Legal pages use their source indexing policy once complete. Robots guidance is not access control. Search, sitemap, related cards, metadata and public route rendering share publication truth.

| Page family | Search intent / keyword ownership | Internal-link requirements | Structured data opportunity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Home/About | Branded discovery/company research | Approved category, solution, work, quote/contact destinations | Truthful Organization; About page context if supported; no invented address/awards |
| Services/category | Broad commercial discovery/comparison | Hub → all eight categories; category → all assigned canonical services | BreadcrumbList; optional truthful list semantics, no rich-result promise |
| Service | Specific commercial need and scope | Parent category; 2–3 actual complementary services; relevant solution; quote | Service and BreadcrumbList using actual visible content |
| Solution | Coordinated business problem | Candidate relationships only as informational links; related solution; quote | Service where semantically appropriate; BreadcrumbList |
| Pricing | Pricing/engagement comparison | Authoritative offer detail; quote; cost FAQs | Ordinary page metadata; do not label uncertain package scope as a binding offer |
| Work/case | Capability/project evaluation | Actual case → demonstrated services; quote | BreadcrumbList; appropriate CreativeWork only if truthful; no fake reviews |
| Insights/article | Informational question | Related reading/help; relevant service/solution; contextual consultation | Article for genuine article content; BreadcrumbList; real author/dates |
| FAQ/help | Purchase/service assistance | Relevant answers/offers; Contact | BreadcrumbList; FAQ markup only if appropriate and current eligibility is verified during implementation, no guaranteed search feature |
| Contact/consultation | Branded contact or inquiry intent | Quote/contact/consultation alternatives | Truthful organization/contact context; no fake appointment availability |
| Utility/policy | Task completion or policy lookup | Relevant recovery/contact/policy links | No fabricated rich-result markup |

No aggregate ratings, partner credentials or fabricated author expertise. Use only actual public facts for Organization metadata. Do not imply a physical local branch or create LocalBusiness locations without evidence. Current search-engine eligibility/markup rules must be verified from primary documentation at implementation; this document preserves source opportunities without asserting current search-result treatment.

Every indexable service body must address its distinct fit, scope, dependencies, process and FAQ. Shared layout is acceptable; wholesale duplicated content is not. One topic should have one useful canonical destination. No new country/language pages, industry pages or product pages are added. Public search includes services, solutions, published articles/resources and FAQ/help only under the approved baseline; no CRM, private evidence or draft content.

GA4/Search Console remain approved directions subject to actual account/domain/consent readiness. Track successful saved inquiry separately from form start, CTA click, consultation request and verified booking. A thank-you page view is not conversion proof. Do not send name/email/phone/message, private IDs, tokens, sensitive query parameters or private-search terms into analytics. Optional replay/attribution tools are not introduced.

<a id="s9-15"></a>

## 15. International content and accessibility conventions

English/USD is the initial presentation. Use country-neutral examples and explicit USD where confusion is possible. Preserve `/month`, `/project`, setup fees and custom qualifications; do not convert currencies or infer tax-inclusive pricing. No exchange-rate service or country-specific price list is approved. Dates should use readable month names when ambiguous numeric dates could confuse; scheduling must retain the actual timezone, and confirmation must use a timezone-aware display. The business is intended to serve international clients, not claimed to have worldwide offices or uniform 24-hour coverage.

R9 writing convention: US-English spelling for consistency, short paragraphs, active verbs, familiar words, defined acronyms and descriptive headings. Use “quote” and “proposal” consistently, with a glossary for unfamiliar technical concepts. Translate meaning rather than idioms in future localization. Keep copy separate from layout, support longer labels, avoid essential text baked into images and record content identity independently of slug/title. No localized routes or hreflang configuration is added before actual approved locale equivalents exist.

Preserve WCAG AA-oriented source requirements: semantic headings/landmarks, keyboard controls, visible focus, sufficient actual contrast, labels/instructions/errors, screen-reader status feedback, captions/transcripts and reduced motion. Decorative images use empty alt; informative visuals describe their relevant meaning; complex diagrams have adjacent text descriptions. Do not keyword-stuff alt text or repeat a nearby caption mechanically. Do not encode price/state solely by color, shape, motion or icon. On mobile, preserve scope, units, limitations and CTA context; avoid tiny image text and swipe-only content.

## 17. Image, media, typography and motion production standards

Preserve the folded-A identity geometry as supplied; recoloring does not authorize a replacement monogram. All three light-surface variants use charcoal lettering; the unchanged symbol geometry follows the selected theme’s primary button color. Inverse mark must be tested on the dark footer; final header choice between compact lockup and text-only remains U4-02. Default Editorial Terracotta uses `#AF432D`; alternate Muted Slate Blue uses `#3F617B`; Vivid Blue uses `#0066FF`; shared page `#F7F4EE`, surface `#FFFFFF`, text `#20201E`, muted text `#625E58`. These are S4 implementation targets, not measured image samples. Keep service icons neutral and consistent. All three are light themes; no full dark mode or fourth palette is added.

Use Inter according to S4 typography and token specifications; do not claim a preview image proves exact font measurements. Only necessary licensed font files/weights/subsets; sensible fallbacks, prompt text display and reserved metrics. No essential headline text embedded into raster artwork. Equal intrinsic dimensions for theme logo/hero variants prevent layout jumps; load only the active large hero eagerly and retain valid art during alternate loading.

| Format/type | Production use | Requirements |
|---|---|---|
| SVG | Logos, consistent icons and exact diagrams | Reviewed/sanitized static assets; preserve viewBox; no untrusted active backend/scripts/external references; accessible text alternative |
| AVIF/WebP | Optimized editorial photos, screenshots and decorative artwork | Compare real quality/size and transparency; avoid unreadable UI text; responsive variants |
| PNG | Lossless working master or transparency/detail where justified | Avoid oversized PNG delivery when an optimized alternative preserves quality |
| JPEG | Compatible photographic source/fallback where appropriate | No transparency expectation; optimize and retain licensed master privately |
| Video | Optional approved project/process evidence, not mandatory hero | Poster, controls, captions/transcript, no autoplay audio, approved rights, bounded loading |
| Motion assets | Selective SVG/layered artwork or authored diagram | Static mobile/reduced-motion fallback; no essential meaning dependent on motion |

Recommended export set R9: 480/768/1200/1600 widths where actual layout needs them; generate only useful variants and use accurate `sizes`. Preserve aspect ratio and width/height; inspect at displayed size. LCP hero is deliberately prioritized, not lazy-loaded; below-fold images lazy-load. Do not preload every asset/font or eagerly fetch all three themes. Crop decorative art freely within approved composition; retain meaningful screenshots/diagram labels in detail views. Redact PII before any public export. File naming: lowercase descriptive kebab-case plus role/theme/size/revision as useful, e.g. `home-hero-terracotta-1200-v1.webp`; illustrative filename only. Keep stable manifest keys distinct from versioned file paths and retain provenance/version/checksum where useful.

**S4 provisional budgets retained:** hero ≤250KB compact / ≤450KB large; initial font transfer ≤150KB; initial marketing transfer ≤1MB before below-fold lazy media/optional tooling; incremental compressed public motion JS ≤60KB on routes using it and zero GSAP payload on unused routes. These remain provisional U4-06 engineering targets, not measured results. Field goals remain LCP ≤2.5s, INP ≤200ms and CLS ≤0.1 at the 75th percentile; no claimed score or conformance result is supplied by this document.

CSS owns simple feedback; Motion for React can own selected small transitions; GSAP is optional and isolated to justified coordinated layered artwork. One owner per animated property. Raster concept art is one layer unless actual separate layers are supplied. No global GSAP, scroll hijacking, mandatory pinned narrative, custom cursor, looping background, WebGL or paid plugin is introduced. Static/reduced-motion content and CTA must remain readable immediately. Theme change is instant or ≤120ms color fade; no scene restart, form reset, scroll jump or large-asset blank state. Clean up listeners/timers/timelines across route/breakpoint/hidden-tab changes. See S4 §7 for exact timings and S6 §9 for module organization.

<a id="s9-18"></a>

## 18. CMS content and database mapping

Do not add collections or change approved schemas to accommodate this document. Planning fields such as review owner, keyword theme and readiness belong in the editorial register unless an existing source field fits. Copy status D/B/V/L is not a new MongoDB enum. Source-backed and database-backed records have one writable master each.

| Content type | Approved owner/model | Fields to populate or preserve | Editing/ownership | Publication/validation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog categories/services/solutions/packages | Validated source records; S5 §8.1 | Exact IDs/names/slugs/memberships, structured sections, related IDs, source priceTerms, scope/availability approval and release version | Catalog module; reviewed Git release; no P1 catalog collections | Public templates/intake share one release; preserve source-label/unit ambiguity |
| Core pages/policies | CorePageContent source records | contentKey/pageId, exact orderedSections, reviewed publicFacts, related keys, version | Content source; owner reviews facts/legal text | Source review/deploy; optional SeoOverride; future editor not inferred |
| Portfolio/cases | CaseStudy source records | contentKey/slug/title/classification, problem/contribution/approach/outcome, permitted assets, related services, publication, SEO/version | Content source; private evidence separate | Draft until actual permission/facts/rights approved; no P1 case collection |
| Articles/resources | Article / articles; S5 §7.6 | Working draft/current published snapshot, stable key/slug, type, author/date/taxonomy/body/SEO/related IDs, publication and revision fields | Content CMS; selected Admin/Editor permission | Explicit save/preview/publish/unpublish; no scheduling/full history |
| Testimonials | Testimonial / testimonials; S5 §7.7 | Quote, attribution, private evidence, optional real case/contact relation, offer IDs, state/order, accountable edits/publication, isDemo | Content CMS; publication permission gate | Granted evidence and genuine content; fictional isDemo production prohibition |
| Source-backed SEO | SeoOverride / seoOverrides; S5 §7.8 | contentKey/pageId, allowed SeoFields, last editor, privileged-change review, concurrency revision | SEO facade delegates to source override | No arbitrary route editing; no weakening reserved noindex/private rules |
| Article/DB-FAQ SEO | Owning content snapshot | SeoFields inside the working/published snapshot | Owning editorial operation via same facade | No duplicate SeoOverride master; draft metadata save is not publish |
| FAQ/help baseline | Source FAQ/help record; S5 §8.1 | Stable key/type/question or title/body, help slug, category, related keys, SEO, visibility/order/version | Source-controlled authoring | Short FAQ anchors only; help detail only for substantive distinct content |
| Optional FAQ editor | FaqEntry / faqEntries; S5 §7.14 | Key/type/slug, workingDraft/publishedSnapshot, state/order/version, publication/editor metadata and searchText | Conditional D-012; replaces source authoring | No simultaneous writable masters; private draft and live public snapshot |
| Topic taxonomy | Source dictionary | topicKey/name/order/type/active | Content taxonomy source | No P1 taxonomy collection or extra category route |
| Assets | Asset manifest + AssetRef; media bytes in controlled storage | Trusted key, visibility, alt/dimensions/MIME/bytes/rights; private provenance | Content controlled manifest; optional upload mode gated | No media binary in MongoDB; no full media library; secure upload controls from first upload |
| Public settings | Source PublicSettings | Brand/descriptor/tagline, selected email/verified contact flags, locale/currency/social links/three themes | Validated configuration | Do not expose operational settings or secrets |
| Inquiry and email copy | Inquiry/OutboxEvent existing operations | Actual submitted identity/type/context/consent and saved reference; controlled template version | Intake/Notifications; not CMS arbitrary templates | Saved truth and minimal escaped email content; no new quote/booking data store |

**Shared field constraints, preserved from S5:** `SeoFields`: title/socialTitle ≤200, description/socialDescription ≤500, canonicalPath ≤512, optional noindex and socialImage AssetRef. `AssetRef`: assetKey ≤300; visibility public/private; alt ≤500 (empty only if decorative); image dimensions when applicable; allowlisted MIME; optional byte count; rightsStatus verified/demo/pending. Pending rights cannot publish. `EditorialBody`: structured allowlisted blocks, ≤1000 blocks and ≤200KiB serialized; no runtime MDX, arbitrary HTML/script/iframe. `ContentSnapshot`: publishable title/body/actual author; optional excerpt ≤1000/image; controlled topics/tags; related existing service/solution IDs; SEO and derived searchText. Tags are bounded to 20 × 60 characters. These are storage/validation contracts, not content-length targets.

**Article:** contentKey; unique slug ≤160; type article/resource; workingDraft; publishedSnapshot; publicationState draft/published/unpublished; publishedVersion; firstPublishedAt; lastPublishedAt/publishedBy; unpublishedAt/unpublishedBy; createdBy/lastEditedBy; published-derived searchText. One working draft and one current public snapshot is the retained R5 recommendation; A5-04/U-03 remains explicit before editor implementation. Save Draft does not publish. Publish validates actual author, body, metadata, assets and relationships. No full revision-history store or scheduling is inferred. Lock published slugs unless an approved redirect migration is performed.

**Testimonial:** contentKey; genuine quote ≤3000; permitted attribution; private Evidence; optional contact/caseStudy relation; related service/solution IDs; state private/published/hidden; displayOrder; accountable publication/edit fields; isDemo. Private evidence is never included in public DTOs, public search, analytics or public media. Fictional production publication prohibited.

**SeoOverride:** unique contentKey; existing pageId; allowlisted fields; editor; high-impact indexing/canonical review metadata; concurrency revision. Applies to registered source-backed pages. Article SEO belongs in its snapshot; optional database FAQ SEO belongs in its snapshot. The SEO facade delegates rather than duplicating metadata. Source defaults return when an override is removed. Reserved utility/private indexing policy cannot be relaxed by editable data.

**FaqEntry, only if selected:** stable contentKey, type short-faq/help-article, conditional unique help slug, workingDraft/publishedSnapshot, publicationState draft/published/hidden, displayOrder, publishedVersion/accountability, searchText. Baseline FAQ/help stays in source. Migration replaces the master rather than operating two editable copies.

**Editorial workflow:** author/editor prepares draft → owner/fact reviewer confirms commercial/claim details → asset rights and accessibility checked → protected preview → authorized publish → verify public page/search/metadata/sitemap/related links → re-review on material change. Exact Admin/Editor publishing rights remain D-013/A5-02. Use existing audit events and revision checks; concurrent edits must not silently overwrite. Unpublish and permission withdrawal immediately remove eligibility even if downstream indexes are stale. Private previews remain authenticated, noindex and excluded from shared caches.

Source-controlled content uses reviewed Git changes/releases; MongoDB content uses explicit authorized operations. Do not seed production with demo fixtures or overwrite editor changes on deployment. No full media manager, page builder, catalog editor, content-history interface or arbitrary settings editor is added to Phase 1.
